compared to QIVc (45,0) for any strain. aQIVc (45,2) also showed statistically significantly higher GMT ratios over aQIVc (45,1) for all 4 strains.

A trend analysis of MN GMTs including QIVc (45,0), aQIVc (45,1) and aQIVc (45,2) has been conducted. Data showed a statistically significant trend with a positive slope for doses QIVc (45,0), aQIVc (45,1) and aQIVc (45,2) for all four strains (slope: 0.050 for A/H1N1 [p-value: 0.0033], 0.050 for A/H3N2 [pvalue: 0.0086], 0.058 for B/Yamagata [p-value: &lt;0.0002], 0.048 for B/Victoria [p-value: 0.0286]).

Percentages of subjects with MN SCRs in general showed an upward trend with higher HA antigen and increasing MF59 dose; the maximum immunogenicity was observed with aQIVc (45,2) formulation for all 4 strains.

Subgroup and Exploratory: Immunogenicity analyses at Days 1 and 29 were stratified by the variables of age group, influenza vaccination history, and baseline titre.

Immunogenicity results, including HI and MN GMTs and GMT ratios, in subjects aged 50 to 64 years were mostly comparable to subjects ≥65 years of age and comparable to the results in the overall population, although immune responses in 50- to 64-year-old subjects were moderately higher in 3 out of 4 strains except for A/H3N2 for aQIVc (45,2). Similar to the overall population, the postvaccination Day 29 HI and MN  GMTs  for  (a)QIVc  formulations  generally  showed  an  upward  trend  with  higher  HA  antigen  and increasing MF59 adjuvant dose for all 4 strains for both age groups. Immunogenicity results, HI GMTs and GMT ratios (GMTr), in subjects aged 50 to 64 years were mostly comparable to subjects ≥65 years of age and comparable to the results in the overall population, with a modestly higher GMTr of aQIVc (45,2) vs QIVc in subjects 50 to 64 years of age as compared to ≥65 years of age for A/H1N1 and B/Victoria. In contrast, aQIVc (45,1) showed a higher HI GMTr for subjects ≥65 years in A/H3N2 and the two B strains. Results from the MN assays showed a similar result. Similar to the overall population, the postvaccination Day 29 HI and MN GMTs for (a)QIVc formulations showed an upward trend with higher HA antigen and increasing MF59 adjuvant dose for all 4 strains. The aQIVc (45,2) dose formulation elicited the best immune response for all 4 strains in both age groups.

In the previously vaccinated subgroup (defined as having received at least one influenza vaccine during the prior 3 years), prevaccination titres were generally higher as compared to not previously vaccinated subgroup on Day 1. The postvaccination titre on Day 29 results showed a pronounced immune response in  terms  of  higher  antibody  titres  in  'not  previously  vaccinated'  subjects  compared  to  'previously vaccinated' subjects in all vaccine groups for all strains, but B/Victoria. In general, the results showed an upward trend in postvaccination titre with higher HA antigen and increasing MF59 dose for all 4 strains, in both subgroups, though more pronounced in the not previously vaccinated subgroup.

The number of subjects with baseline HI and MN titre &lt;1:10 was small. Based on HI and MN titres, with the exception of B/Victoria strain, all other strains showed higher post vaccination GMT ratios in subjects with low baseline titre as compared to subjects with a baseline titre ≥1:10.

The exploratory objective was to measure immune response of study vaccines at different HI titre cutoff levels that showed more pronounced difference between the vaccine groups.

aQIVc (45,2) showed the highest percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:80, ≥1:160 and ≥1:320 for all 4 strains. In general, a greater difference in percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:80, ≥1:160 and ≥1:320 was observed with aQIVc (45,2) vs the lower doses compared with the differences between the lower aQIVc formulations.

In summary, the immunogenicity results demonstrated that increasing doses of HA antigen to 45 µg of HA  per  strain  and  MF59  adjuvant  to  2x  standard  dose  [aQIVc(45,2)]  elicited  the  highest  immune response in subjects aged 50 years or older and therefore aQIVc (45,2) was confirmed to be the most immunogenic dose formulation and was chosen for further testing in pivotal study V201\\_03.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.3.2. Main study

## 5.3.2.1. Study V201\\_03

## 5.3.2.1.1. Study title

A Phase 3, Observer-Blind, Controlled, Multicentre, Clinical Study to Evaluate Immunogenicity and Safety of  a  High-Dose MF59-Adjuvanted Quadrivalent Subunit Cell-derived Influenza Vaccine (aQIVc HD) in Comparison with a Non-adjuvanted Quadrivalent Recombinant Influenza Vaccine (QIVr) and an MF59Adjuvanted Quadrivalent Subunit Egg-derived Influenza Vaccine (aQIVc), in Adults Aged 50 Years and Older.

## 5.3.2.1.2. Study design

Study V201\\_03 was a parallel-group, randomized, comparator-controlled, observer-blind, multicentre study of immunogenicity and safety in male and female adults aged 50 years and older (approximately equally split between two age groups: 50-64 years; 65 years and older), who were healthy or had stable comorbidities that increased their risk of complications from influenza infection. Three lots of aQIVc HD were evaluated for consistency and pooled for the comparison with the two control vaccines (QIVr; Flublok Quadrivalent/Supemtek and aQIV; Fluad Quadrivalent/Tetra).

Study V201\\_03 aimed to demonstrate the immunological equivalence of 3 consecutive production lots of aQIVc HD (lot-to-lot consistency) and to compare the immunogenicity and safety of aQIVc HD versus two influenza vaccines, QIVr and aQIV, up to 12 months following vaccination. Immunogenicity, safety, and exploratory endpoints were assessed in the overall study population (adults aged 50 years and older) and in the age subgroups 50-64 years and 65 years and older. Immunogenicity and safety data from this study were to be used to support the licensure of aTIVc for use in adults aged 50 years and older.

Study V201\\_03 was conducted in 7699 male and female subjects. Subjects were randomly assigned to receive 1 of 3 lots of aQIVc HD, QIVr, or aQIV in a 1:1:1:2:2 ratio (for a 3:2:2 ratio for aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV), with stratification factors for age group (50-64 years; 65 years and older), history of any influenza vaccination within the previous 3 influenza seasons (yes; no), and site; an equal distribution of age groups was maintained on study level and in Long-Term (LT) subset and cell-mediated immunity (CMI) subset.

The HI-Egg subset of 3803 subjects was to include 70% of the total enrolled subjects randomly selected among subjects exposed to aQIVc HD or aQIV.

The  Long-Term  immunogenicity  subset  of  774  subjects  was  selected  via  convenience  sampling  in selected US sites.

The CMI subset of approximately 100 subjects was selected from the LT immunogenicity subset at chosen sites via convenience sampling (non-probability sampling method that involves selection of subjects on no other criteria than being available and willing to participate). The CMI analyses are currently ongoing; the results will be reported in a subsequent version of the CSR.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Figure 1. Study schema

<!-- image -->

## 5.3.2.1.2.1. Treatment

## Investigational vaccine:

aQIVc HD: Adjuvanted Quadrivalent Subunit Inactivated Cell-derived Influenza Vaccine, which includes 45 μ g  of  each  HA  antigen  and  2x  MF59  adjuvant  (19.5  mg  of  squalene)  in  1.0  mL  of  the  vaccine, formulated in a PFS. The HA antigens are derived from the 4 influenza virus strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria).

## Comparator vaccine:

QIVr: Non-adjuvanted Quadrivalent Recombinant Influenza Vaccine (QIVr), which includes 45 μ g of each HA antigen in 0.5 mL of the vaccine, formulated in a PFS. The HA antigens are derived from the 4 influenza virus strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria).

aQIV: Adjuvanted Quadrivalent Subunit Inactivated Egg-derived Influenza Vaccine, which was prepared from viruses grown in embryonated eggs and includes 15 μ g of each HA antigen and MF59 adjuvant (containing 9.75 mg of squalene) in 0.5 mL of the vaccine, formulated in a PFS. The HA antigens are derived from the 4 influenza virus strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria).

## Treatment groups:

aQIVc HD group: approximately 3300 subjects receiving 1 of 3 lots of aQIVc HD (N=1100 for each lot)

QIVr group: approximately 2200 subjects

aQIV group: approximately 2200 subjects

All treatment groups included subjects aged 50 years and older. All subjects received one dose of study vaccine at Day 1.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.3.2.1.2.2. Randomisation

Subjects were randomly assigned to receive 1 of 3 lots of aQIVc HD, or 1 of the 2 comparator vaccines, QIVr or aQIV, in a 1:1:1:2:2 ratio (for a 3:2:2 ratio for aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV). An Interactive Response Technology (IRT) system was used for subject randomization, with stratification factors for age group (50-64 years; 65 years and older), history of any influenza vaccination within the previous 3 influenza seasons (yes; no), and site; the capping functionality in the IRT system was used to control an equal distribution of age groups on the study level and in the Long-Term and CMI subsets.

- The Long-Term subset of approximately 770 subjects (for immunogenicity) was selected via convenience sampling.
- The HI-Egg subset of approximately 3850 subjects included 70% of the total enrolled subjects randomly selected among subjects exposed to aQIVc HD or aQIV.
- The CMI subset of approximately 100 subjects was selected from the Long-Term subset at chosen sites via convenience sampling.

## 5.3.2.1.2.3. Blinding

Observer-blind study.

## 5.3.2.1.2.4. Patient population

The planned study population was approximately 7700 male and female adults aged 50 years and older (approximately equally  split  between  two  age  groups:  50-64  years;  65  years  and  older),  who  were healthy or had stable comorbidities that increase their risk of complications from influenza infection.

## Main Inclusion Criteria:

In order to participate in this study, all subjects must have met ALL of the inclusion criteria described below:

1. Individuals ≥50 years of age on the day of informed consent.

2. Healthy individuals or individuals with stable comorbidities which increase their risk of complications from influenza infection.

## Main Exclusion Criteria:

In order to participate in this study, all subjects must not have met ANY of the exclusion criteria described below:

1. Females who were pregnant or lactating, or females of childbearing potential who had not adhered to a specified set of contraceptive methods from at least 30 days prior to study entry and who did not plan to do so until 2 months after the study vaccination.

2. Progressive, unstable, or uncontrolled clinical conditions.

3.  Hypersensitivity,  including  allergy,  to  any  component  of  vaccines  whose  use  was  foreseen in this study.

4. Known history of Guillain-Barré syndrome or another demyelinating disease such as encephalomyelitis and transverse myelitis.

5. Clinical conditions representing a contraindication to intramuscular administration of vaccines or blood draws.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

6. Abnormal function of the immune system, unless evaluated as stable within the 90 days prior to informed consent, per clinical and biological assessments.

7. Radiotherapy within 90 days prior to informed consent.

8. Systemic administration of corticosteroids at a dose of ≥20 mg/day of prednisone or equivalent for more than 14 consecutive days within 90 days prior to informed consent.

9. Administration of antineoplastic agents or immunomodulating agents within 90 days prior to informed consent.

10.  Receipt  of  polyclonal  immunoglobulins  or  any  blood  products  within  180  days  prior  to  informed consent.

11. Planned use of an investigational product other than the study vaccine.

12. Receipt of any influenza vaccine within 180 days prior to informed consent or planned to receive an influenza vaccine within 9 months from study vaccination for all subjects and 12 months from study vaccination for subjects assigned to the Long-Term subset for immunogenicity.

13. Receipt of any COVID-19 vaccine within 14 days (non-replicating vaccines) or 28 days (replicating vaccines)  prior  to  enrollment  or  plan  to  receive  any  COVID-19  vaccine  within  7  days  from  study vaccination.

14. Receipt of any other vaccines within 14 days (for inactivated vaccines) or 28 days (for live vaccines) prior to enrollment in this study or who were planning to receive any other vaccine within 28 days from study vaccination.

15. Terminal illness or an existing end-stage disease with &lt;12 months life expectancy.

16. Study personnel or immediate family or household member of study personnel.

17. Any other clinical condition that, in the opinion of the Investigator, might interfere with the results of the study or pose additional risk to the subject due to participation in the study.

## 5.3.2.1.3. Objectives and estimands

## 5.3.2.1.3.1. Primary objective

The primary immunogenicity objectives were:

- To demonstrate clinical lot-to-lot consistency of 3 consecutive aQIVc HD lots, in terms of immunogenicity for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 50 years and older, as measured by hemagglutination inhibition (HI) assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.
- Success criteria: Lot-to-lot consistency would be claimed if the 2-sided 95% confidence intervals (CIs) for the Day 29 ratio of geometric means between each pair among the 3 lots are entirely contained within the prespecified equivalence range (0.67 to 1.5) for each of the 4 vaccine strains.
- To demonstrate immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs aQIV for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 50 years and older, as measured by HI GMTs and SCRs, using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.
- Success criteria: Noninferiority would be demonstrated if the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/aQIV) is ≥ 0.67 for each of the 4 vaccine strains, and the LL of

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the difference in SCR (aQIVc HD minus aQIV) is ≥ -10% for each of the 4 vaccine strains.

- To demonstrate immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs QIVr for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 50 years and older, as measured by HI geometric mean titres (GMTs) and seroconversion rates (SCRs), using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29. For the purpose of US licensure, only subjects enrolled in countries where QIVr Flublok is used as a comparator (US, Canada, Pakistan) are included in this analysis.
- Success criteria: Noninferiority would be demonstrated if the lower limit (LL) of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/QIVr) is ≥0.67 for each of the 4 vaccine strains, and the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the difference in SCR (aQIVc HD minus QIVr) is ≥ -10% for each of the 4 vaccine strains.
- * For the purpose of US licensure only, subjects enrolled in countries where Flublok is used as a comparator will be included in this analysis.

## 5.3.2.1.3.2. Estimands for the primary objective

## Clinical Lot-To-Lot Consistency

Immunogenicity of 3 lots of aQIVc HD at Day 29 were compared in pairs via HI assay using cell-derived target viruses:

The following ratio of Day 29 GMTs are reported with the associated 2-sided 95% CIs:

- aQIVc HD Lot 1 / aQIVc HD Lot 2
- aQIVc HD Lot 1 / aQIVc HD Lot 3
- aQIVc HD Lot 2 / aQIVc HD Lot 3

## Noninferiority Endpoints

Humoral immune responses of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV vaccines, in terms of HI antibody response against homologous vaccine strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria), using cell-derived target viruses, was assessed by:

- GMT: Geometric mean of antibody titres at Day 29
- SCR: Percentage of subjects with seroconversion (defined as either a prevaccination [Day 1] titre &lt;1:10 and a postvaccination [Day 29] titre ≥ 1:40, or a prevaccination titre ≥ 1:10 and a ≥ 4-fold increase in postvaccination titre)

Noninferiority  of  aQIVc  HD  versus  QIVr  and  aQIV  was  assessed  via  Day  29  GMT  ratio  (aQIVc HD/comparator,  where  the  comparator  is  QIVr  or  aQIV)  and  SCR  difference  (aQIVc  HD  minus comparator) for each virus strain included in the vaccines, using cell-derived target viruses, via the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 97.5% CIs:

- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator) for the A/H1N1 strain
- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator) for the A/H3N2 strain
- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator) for the B strain (Yamagata lineage)
- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator) for the B strain (Victoria lineage)
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator) for the A/H1N1 strain
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator) for the A/H3N2 strain

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator) for the B strain (Yamagata lineage)
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator) for the B strain (Victoria lineage)

## 5.3.2.1.3.3. Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on primary estimands

## Statistical Hypothesis

All primary immunogenicity analyses were conducted in the PPS Immunogenicity in a sequential manner on the immunogenicity data collected up to Day 29. First, the lot-to-lot consistency was tested. After confirmation of lot-to-lot consistency (ie, rejection of all null hypotheses), the noninferiority testing of aQIVc HD versus each of the two comparator vaccines (QIVr and aQIV) was conducted.

## Lot-to-Lot Consistency

For each of the 4 vaccine influenza strains, the following hypotheses were tested:

```
H01: μ lot1 ≠ μ lot2 versus Ha1: μ lot1 = μ lot2 H02: μ lot1 ≠ μ lot3 versus Ha2: μ lot1 = μ lot3 H03: μ lot2 ≠ μ lot3 versus Ha3: μ lot2 = μ lot3
```

where μlot1, μlot2, and μlot3 denote the Day 29 GMTs for subjects receiving Lot 1, Lot 2, and Lot 3, respectively, of aQIVc HD. H0x refers to the null hypothesis of nonequivalence (inconsistency), ie, that the 2-sided 95% CI for the GMT ratio for each pair of lots was outside the equivalence range (0.67 to 1.5). Hax refers to the alternative hypothesis of equivalence (consistency), ie, that the 2-sided 95% CI on the GMT ratio was entirely contained within the equivalence range (0.67 to 1.5).

For  each  pair  of  lots,  the  GMT  ratio  and  their  2-sided  95%  CIs  were  calculated  separately  for  each influenza strain. Lot-to-lot consistency was confirmed if for each of the 4 vaccine strains the 2-sided 95% CIs for the ratios of geometric means between each pair among the 3 lots were entirely contained within the equivalence range of 0.67 to 1.5.

The  significance  level  for  all  these  tests  was  α=0.05  (2 -sided),  which  needed  no  adjustment  for multiplicity because all 12 null hypotheses had to be rejected.

## Noninferiority Analysis

After confirmation of lot-to-lot consistency for aQIVc HD, noninferiority testing was performed versus each comparator vaccine (QIVr and aQIV) and the following parallel hypotheses were tested for each of the 4 vaccine strains:

```
H01: μ aQIVc HD / μ comparator <0.67 versus Ha1: μ aQIVc HD / μ comparator ≥ 0.67 H02: π aQIVc HD - π comparator <-10% versus Ha2: π aQIVc HD - π comparator ≥
```

-10%

where μaQIVc HD and μcomparator denote the Day 29 GMTs and πaQIVc HD and πcomparator denote the SCRs for aQIVc HD and comparator vaccines (QIVr and aQIV), respectively.

To assess noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs QIVr, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI was compared with the prespecified thresholds, namely:

- The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the ratio of the Day 29 GMTs met or exceeded 0.67. The GMT ratio was calculated as GMT (aQIVc HD) divided by GMT (QIVr).
- The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the difference between the Day 29 SCRs met or exceeded - 10%. The difference in SCRs was calculated as SCR (aQIVc HD) minus SCR (QIVr).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Noninferiority of aQIVc compared to QIVr was additionally assessed in the subset of subjects enrolled in countries using a US-licensed vaccine for QIVr (US, Canada, and Pakistan).

Same analysis model and same hypothesis testing applied to all countries subjects was used to assess noninferiority in this subset.

In the same way, to assess noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs aQIV, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI was compared with the prespecified thresholds, namely:

- The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the ratio of the Day 29 GMTs met or exceeded 0.67. The GMT ratio was calculated as GMT (aQIVc HD) divided by GMT (aQIV).
- The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the difference between the Day 29 SCRs met or exceeded - 10%. The difference in SCRs was calculated as SCR (aQIVc HD) minus SCR (aQIV).

For  the  evaluation  of  noninferiority  (primary  objective),  an  alpha  adjustment  using  the  Bonferroni method  was  applied  and  each  test  was  performed  at  the  0.025  (2-sided)  significance  level  (ie, α/2=0.0125, 1 -sided).

## Handling of missing values for immunogenicity data

For  immunogenicity  data,  it  is  reasonable  to  consider  missing  immunogenicity  values  as  missing completely  at  random,  i.e.,  not  informative.  Therefore,  the  immunogenicity  analysis  comprised  a complete case analysis only, without introducing any bias. Imputation methods were not used.

## 5.3.2.1.3.4. Secondary objectives

The key secondary immunogenicity objectives were:

- To demonstrate immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs aQIV for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 65 years and older, as measured by HI GMTs and SCRs, using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.
- To compare immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs aQIV for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 50 years and older, in terms of superiority as measured by HI assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.
- To compare immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs QIVr for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 50 years and older, in terms of superiority as measured by HI assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.
- To compare immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs aQIV for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 65 years and older, in terms of superiority as measured by HI assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.

The other secondary immunogenicity objectives were:

- To compare immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs QIVr for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 50 years and older, in terms of superiority by margin as measured by HI assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.
- To compare immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs aQIV for each vaccine strain in subjects aged 65 years and older, in terms of superiority by margin as measured by HI assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 29.
- To assess the immunogenicity of aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV for each homologous vaccine strain, in all subjects, overall and by age subgroup (50-64 years; 65 years and older), as measured by HI assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 1 and Day 29.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- To assess the immunogenicity of aQIVc HD in comparison with aQIV for each homologous vaccine strain, in a subset of at least 3850 subjects (approximately 70% of the total enrolled aQIVc HD and aQIV subjects), overall and by age subgroup (50-64 years; 65 years and older), as measured by HI assay using egg-derived target viruses, at Day 1 and Day 29.
- To assess the long-term immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs QIVr and aQIV for each homologous vaccine strain, in a subset of approximately 770 subjects aged 50 years and older, overall and by age subgroup (50-64 years; 65 years and older), as measured by HI assay using cellderived target viruses, up to Day 365.
- To assess the immunogenicity of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV for each homologous vaccine strain, in a subset of approximately 770 subjects aged 50 years and older, as measured by virus microneutralization (MN) assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 1 and Day 29.
- To assess the long-term immunogenicity of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV for each homologous vaccine strain, in a subset of approximately 770 subjects aged 50 years and older, as measured by virus microneutralization (MN) assay using cell-derived target viruses, up to Day 365.

## 5.3.2.1.3.5. Estimands for the secondary objectives

## Key Secondary Immunogenicity Endpoints:

Once  noninferiority  of  aQIVc  HD  versus  aQIV  in  subjects  aged  50  years  and  older  is  confirmed, noninferiority was evaluated to compare aQIVc HD versus aQIV in subjects aged 65 years and older against homologous vaccine strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria) with associated 2sided adjusted CIs:

- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/aQIV): Geometric mean ratio of HI antibody titre at Day 29
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus aQIV): Difference of the percentage of subjects with seroconversion at Day 29

Statistical superiority of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr in subjects aged 50 years and older, and statistical superiority of aQIVc HD in comparison with aQIV in subjects aged 50 years and older and in subjects  aged  65  years  and  older,  were  evaluated  using  HI  antibody  response  at  Day  29,  against homologous vaccine strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria) with associated 2-sided adjusted CIs:

- GMT ratio of HI antibody titre at Day 29

Other Secondary Immunogenicity Endpoints:

## Cell-derived Target Viruses HI Antibody Response Assessment

Superiority by margin of aQIVc HD versus QIVr in subjects aged 50 years and older and superiority by margin of aQIVc HD versus aQIV in subjects aged 65 years and older were evaluated at Day 29 using cell-derived target viruses via GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator) with associated 2-sided 97.5% CIs.

Humoral immune response of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV vaccines was evaluated in terms of HI antibody response at Day 29 in all subjects against homologous vaccine strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria) in the overall population (ie, subjects aged 50 years and older) and  by  age  group  (ie,  50-64  years;  65  years  and  older),  using  cell-derived  target  viruses,  via  the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 95% CIs (as defined in the final Statistical Analysis Plan [SAP; Version 4.0, 30 August 2024]):

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- GMT: Geometric mean of antibody titres at Day 1 and Day 29
- GMFI: Geometric mean of the fold increase in postvaccination (Day 29) compared to prevaccination (Day 1) antibody titres
- Percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥ 1:40 at Day 1 and Day 29
- Percentage of subjects with seroconversion at Day 29

Group comparisons:

- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator)
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator)

## Egg-derived Target Viruses HI Antibody Response Assessment

Humoral immune response of aQIVc HD in comparison with aQIV vaccine was evaluated in terms of HI antibody  response  (Day  29)  using  egg-derived  target  viruses,  against  homologous  vaccine  strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria) in a subset of approximately 3850 subjects (at least 70% of the total enrolled aQIVc HD and aQIV subjects), in the overall age population (ie, 50 years and older) and by age group (ie, 50-64 years; 65 years and older), via the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 95% CIs:

- GMT: Geometric mean of antibody titres at Day 1 and Day 29
- GMFI: Geometric mean of the fold increase in postvaccination (Day 29) from prevaccination (Day 1) antibody titres
- Percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥ 1:40 at Day 1 and Day 29
- Percentage of subjects with seroconversion at Day 29

Group comparisons:

- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/aQIV)
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus aQIV)

## Cell-derived Target Viruses Long-Term HI Antibody Response Assessment

The long-term humoral immune response of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV vaccines was evaluated in terms of HI antibody response at Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365 in a subset of approximately 770 subjects, against homologous vaccine strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria) in the overall population and by age group (ie, 50-64 years; 65 years and older), using cellderived target viruses, via the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 95% CIs:

- GMT: Geometric mean of antibody titres at Day 1, Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365
- GMFI: Geometric mean of the fold increase in postvaccination (Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365) compared to prevaccination (Day 1) antibody titres
- Percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥ 1:40 at Day 1, Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365
- Percentage of subjects with seroconversion at Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365

Group comparisons:

- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator)
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Cell-derived (Homologous) Target Viruses MN Antibody Response Assessment

The humoral immune response of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV vaccines, in terms of MN antibody response at Day 29 against homologous vaccine strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria), was evaluated at Day 29 in a subset of approximately 770 subjects, using cell-derived target viruses, via the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 95% CIs:

- GMT: Geometric mean of antibody titres at Day 1 and Day 29
- GMFI: Geometric mean of the fold increase in postvaccination (Day 29) from prevaccination (Day 1) antibody titres
- Percentage of subjects with seroconversion (defined as either a prevaccination MN titre &lt; lower limit of quantitation [LLOQ] and a postvaccination MN titre ≥ 1:[4xLLOQ], or a prevaccination MN titre ≥ LLOQ and a ≥ 4-fold increase in postvaccination MN titre) at Day 29

Group comparisons:

- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator)
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator)

## Cell-derived (Homologous) Target Viruses Long-Term MN Antibody Response Assessment

The long-term humoral immune response of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV vaccines in terms of MN antibody response up to Day 365, against homologous vaccine strains (A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria), was evaluated in a subset of approximately 770 subjects, using cell-derived target viruses, via the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 95% CIs:

- GMT: Geometric mean of antibody titres at Day 1, Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365
- GMFI: Geometric mean of the fold increase in postvaccination (Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365) compared to prevaccination (Day 1) antibody titres
- Percentage of subjects with seroconversion (defined as either a prevaccination MN titre &lt;LLOQ and a postvaccination MN titre ≥ 1:[4xLLOQ], or a prevaccination MN titre ≥ LLOQ and a ≥ 4-fold increase in postvaccination MN titre) at Day 29, Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365
- GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/comparator)
- SCR difference (aQIVc HD minus comparator)

## 5.3.2.1.3.6. Statistical  methods  for  estimation  and  sensitivity  analysis  on  the  secondary estimands

The FAS Immunogenicity was used for key secondary immunogenicity analyses of statistical superiority. The PPS Immunogenicity was used for key secondary immunogenicity analysis of noninferiority.

## Statistical Hypotheses

Noninferiority Analyses in Subjects 65 Years and Older

The same analysis model planned for primary noninferiority endpoints was applied for aQIVc HD versus aQIV comparisons in the subset of subjects aged 65 years and older, excluding age group from the list of covariates.

The following hypotheses were tested in subjects aged 65 years and old, for each of the 4 vaccine strains:

H01: μ aQIVc(65) HD / μ aQIV(65) &lt;0.67 versus Ha1: μ aQIVc(65) HD / μ aQIV(65) ≥ 0.67

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

```
H02: π aQIVc(65) HD - π aQIV(65) <-10% versus Ha2: π aQIVc(65) HD - π caQIV(65) ≥ -10%
```

where μaQIVc(65) HD and μaQIV(65) denote the Day 29 GMTs and πaQIVc(65) HD and πaQIV(65) denote the SCR for aQIVc HD and aQIV, respectively in subjects aged 65 years and older.

## Superiority Analyses

The superiority test was conducted following noninferiority within the same comparator and within the same age group.

For each of the 4 vaccine strains, in subjects aged 50 years and older, the following statistical hypotheses were tested:

```
H0: μ aQIVc HD / μ QIVr ≤ 1 versus Ha: μ aQIVc HD / μ QIVr >1
```

where μaQIVc HD and μQIVr denote the Day 29 GMTs for aQIVc HD and QIVr, respectively.

Superiority of aQIVc HD vs QIVr was to be concluded if the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the ratio of the Day 29 GMTs exceeded 1.0 for each vaccine strain.

To evaluate superiority of aQIVc HD versus aQIV, after noninferiority confirmation in subjects 65 years and older, the following statistical hypotheses were tested in parallel applying Bonferroni-Holm procedure for multiplicity adjustment:

```
H0: μ aQIVc(50) HD / μ aQIV(50) ≤ 1.0 versus Ha: μ aQIVc(50) HD / μ aQIV(50) >1.0 H0: μ aQIVc(65) HD / μ aQIV(65) ≤ 1.0 versus Ha: μ aQIVc(65) HD / μ aQIV(65) >1.0
```

where μaQIVc HD and μaQIV denote the Day 29 GMTs for aQIVc HD and aQIV, respectively.

Superiority by Margin Analyses

Superiority by a margin of aQIVc HD against QIVr would be tested if statistical superiority (key secondary endpoints) was confirmed. The following statistical hypothesis was to be tested:

```
H0: μ aQIVc HD / μ QIVr ≤ 1.5 versus Ha: μ aQIVc HD / μ QIVr >1.5
```

Superiority by margin would be concluded if the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/QIVr) was &gt;1.5 for at least 1 of the 2 subtype A strains.

In the same way, superiority by a margin of aQIVc HD against aQIV would be tested in subjects aged 65 years  and  older  if  statistical  superiority  for  the  same  comparator  and  age  group  (key  secondary endpoints) was confirmed. The following statistical hypothesis was to be tested:

```
H0: μ aQIVc HD(65) / μ aQIV(65) ≤ 1.5 versus Ha: μ aQIVc HD(65) / μ aQIV(65) >1.5
```

Superiority by margin would be demonstrated if the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/aQIV) was &gt;1.5 for at least 1 of the 2 subtype A strains.

## 5.3.2.1.3.7. Exploratory objectives

The exploratory objectives were:

1. To characterize the cell-mediated immune response of aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV in a subset of approximately 100 subjects aged 50 years and older, at Day 1, Day 8, Day 15, Day 29 and Day 181.
2. To characterize the anti-neuraminidase (NA) immunogenicity of aQIVc HD, QIVr and aQIV for each vaccine strain, in a subset of approximately 100 subjects aged 50 years and older, as measured by enzyme-linked lectin assay (ELLA), at Day 1 and Day 29.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

3. To assess the immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs aQIV for each homologous vaccine strain, in the LongTerm subset, aged 50 years and older, overall and by age subgroup (50-64 years; 65 years), as measured by HI assay using egg-derived target viruses, up to Day 365.
4. To  assess  the  immunogenicity  of  aQIVc  HD  in  comparison  with  QIVr  and  aQIV  for  A/H3N2 heterologous vaccine strains, in a subset of approximately 770 subjects aged 50 years and older, as measured by MN assay using cell-derived target viruses, at Day 1 and up to Day 365.

## 5.3.2.1.3.8. Estimands for the exploratory objectives

1. CMI assessment: antigen-specific CD4+ and CD8+ T-cell responses of the aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV vaccines will be measured by intracellular cytokine staining and flow cytometry in the established time points, as measured by numbers of cells expressing cytokines such as (but not limited to) interferon  gamma  (IFNγ ),  interleukin  2  (IL-2),  tumor  necrosis  factor  alpha  (TNFα ),  and  other potential markers.
2. Anti-NA antibody response of aQIVc HD, QIVr and aQIV vaccines against the homologous NA for each  virus  in  the  vaccine  strains  will  be  assessed  by  ELLA  at  Day  1  and  Day  29:  GMT,  GMFI, percentage of subjects with a ≥ 4-fold increase in postvaccination ELLA titre from Day 1 and GMT ratio.
3. The  long-term  humoral  immune  response  of  aQIVc  HD  in  comparison  with  aQIV  vaccine  was evaluated in terms of HI antibody response, against homologous vaccine strains, up to day 365, using egg-derived target virus, via the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 95% CIs:GMT, GMFI, percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥ 1:40, percentage of subjects with seroconversion, GMT ratio, SCR difference.
4. Humoral immune response of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV vaccines in terms of MN antibody response was evaluated up to Day 365, against A/H3N2 heterologous vaccine strains, in a subset of approximately 770 subjects, using cell-derived target viruses, via the following estimates and the associated 2-sided 95% CIs:GMT, GMFI, percentage of subjects with seroconversion, GMT ratio and SCR difference.

## 5.3.2.1.3.9. Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on the exploratory estimands

Immune responses of aQIVc HD in comparison with QIVr and aQIV vaccines were additionally evaluated in  the  overall  population  and  by  age  subgroup  (50-64  years;  65  years  and  older)  using  descriptive statistics based on cell-derived HI antibody titres at Day 1 and Day 29, GMFI at Day 29, the percentage of subjects with seroconversion at Day 29, and the percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥ 1:40.

The same statistical model as the one used for the primary and noninferiority objectives was to be applied to compute difference across groups in terms of the GMT ratio and SCR difference.

The immune response at Day 29 was to be additionally evaluated using different laboratory tests such as MN and HI assays using egg-derived antigen.

## 5.3.2.1.4. Results

## 5.3.2.1.4.1. Participant flow and numbers analysed

A total of 7699 subjects aged 50 years and older were randomized in the study, with the majority of subjects (7677 subjects, 99.7%) receiving study vaccine. The remaining 22 subjects did not receive study vaccine because of screen failure for 11 subjects, 8 subjects withdrew their consent, 2 subjects were randomized in error, and 1 subject was a regular plasma donor who was erroneously withdrawn.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The majority of subjects (97.6%) completed the study up to Day 181, with 186 of 7699 subjects (2.4%) discontinuing from the study before Day 181. The most common reasons for discontinuing from the study before Day 181 were lost to follow-up (1.2% of subjects) and withdrawal of consent (0.7% of subjects).

The  majority  of  subjects  (80.2%)  completed  the  study  up  to  Day  365,  with  1408  of  7699  subjects (18.3%) not reconsenting for 12 months safety follow-up and 120 of 7699 subjects (1.6%) discontinuing from the study before Day 365; these 120 subjects were in the Long-Term subset or signed reconsent for the 12-month safety follow-up. The most common reasons for discontinuing from the study before Day 365 were lost to follow-up (0.9% of subjects) and withdrawal of consent (0.5% of subjects).

## Datasets Analysed

The All Randomized Set consisted of the 7699 subjects who were randomized in the study. In total, 81 subjects were excluded from the Full Analysis Set (FAS) Immunogenicity: 59 subjects did not have HI immunogenicity data available at either Day 1 or Day 29, and 22 subjects did not receive study vaccine. An  additional  199  subjects  were  excluded  from  the  PPS  Immunogenicity,  188  subjects  for  protocol deviations (PDs) (most commonly for not complying with the blood draw schedule [133 subjects]) and 11 subjects for an unplanned concomitant vaccination before Day 29.

The  FAS-HI-Egg  included  3803  subjects,  approximately  70%  of  the  total  enrolled  subjects  in  the aQIVc HD and aQIV groups who were randomly selected for HI testing using egg-derived target viruses; the  associated  PPS-HI-Egg  included  3696  subjects.  The  FAS-MN  included  755  subjects  who  were randomly assigned to the Long-Term subset and tested for MN antibody response using cell-derived target viruses; the associated PPS-MN included 713 subjects.

The Long-Term subset consisted of 774 subjects selected via convenience sampling. In total, 42 subjects were excluded from the FAS-HI-LT: 37 subjects did not have HI immunogenicity data available at Day 1 or any of the long-term time points postvaccination (ie, Day 181, Day 271, Day 365), and 5 subjects did not receive study vaccine. An additional 103 subjects were excluded from the PPS-HI-LT. Thus, a total of 732 subjects and 629 subjects were included in the FAS-HI-LT and PPS-HI-LT, respectively.

## 5.3.2.1.4.2. Deviations from study plan

Overall, 505 of 7677 subjects (6.6%) had a major PD. Not all major PDs were reason to exclude subjects from (one of) the analysis sets. The most common major PDs were related to laboratory assessments (serum sample not collected, Day 29 visit not done, or serum sample collected outside visit window; 3.1% of subjects), informed consent and process (2.0% of subjects), and safety (subjects who did not enter any reactogenicity data in the eDiary due to technical issues or otherwise [and hence were excluded from the Solicited Safety Set]; 0.9% of subjects).

The  proportion  of  subjects  reporting  major  PDs  during  the  study  was  low  and  did  not  affect  the conclusions of the analyses.

## 5.3.2.1.4.3. Baseline data

## Medical History and Concurrent Illnesses

At least 1 medical disorder was reported as medical history for 6259 of 7677 subjects (81.5%) in the All Exposed Set The percentage of subjects with at least 1 medical disorder was similar between the aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV vaccine groups (81.2%, 82.7%, and 80.9%, respectively).

## Prior and Concomitant Medications

At least 1 prior medication was reported for 6450 of 7699 subjects (83.8%) in the All Randomized Set. The percentage of subjects reporting use of prior medication was similar between the aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV vaccine groups (83.6%, 83.7%, and 84.0%, respectively).

At least 1 concomitant medication was reported for 6152 of 7677 subjects (80.1%) from Day 1 to Day 181 in the All Exposed Set. The percentage of subjects reporting use of concomitant medication was similar between the aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV vaccine groups (80.9%, 79.2%, and 79.9%, respectively).

At least 1 concomitant medication was reported for 4691 of 6246 subjects (75.1%) from Day 182 to Day 365 in the 12 Months Safety Set. The percentage of subjects reporting use of concomitant medication was  similar  between  the  aQIVc  HD,  QIVr,  and  aQIV  vaccine  groups  (75.1%,  75.4%,  and  74.9%, respectively).

## 5.3.2.1.4.4. Outcomes and estimation

## Primary Immunogenicity Results

## Lot-to-lot Consistency

The 2-sided 95% CIs for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratios for each of the lot-to-lot pairwise comparisons are entirely contained within the prespecified equivalence range (0.67 to 1.5) for each of the A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria strains (Table 15, Figure 3).

Therefore,  the  prespecified  success  criteria  for  demonstrating  clinical  lot-to-lot  consistency  of  3 consecutive aQIVc HD lots were met in subjects aged 50 years and older.

Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs QIVr in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older

Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs QIVr (Flublok Quadrivalent/Supemtek) in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older

## o GMT Ratio

The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was ≥ 0.67 for 3 of the 4 vaccine strains (A/H1N1: 0.87; B/Yamagata: 1.27; B/Victoria: 1.33) (Table 16, Figure 4). The LL was marginally below 0.67 for the A/H3N2 strain (0.65).

## o SCR Difference

The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% for 3 of the 4 vaccine strains (A/H1N1: -1.2%; B/Yamagata: 10.3%; B/Victoria: 8.5%) (Table 16). The LL was marginally below - 10% for the A/H3N2 strain (-10.3%).

- o Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs QIVr (Flublok Quadrivalent/Supemtek)

The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was ≥ 0.67 and the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% for the A/H1N1, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria strains, but not the A/H3N2 strain. Therefore, the prespecified success criteria for demonstrating immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs QIVr were not met in subjects aged 50 years and older.

Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs US-Licensed QIVr (Flublok Quadrivalent) in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older (US/Canada/Pakistan)

## o GMT Ratio

The LL of the 2sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was ≥0.67 for 3 of the 4 vaccine strains (A/H1N1: 0.86; B/Yamagata: 1.23; B/Victoria: 1.34) (Table 17, Figure 5). The LL was marginally below 0.67 for the A/H3N2 strain (0.66).

## o SCR Difference

The LL of the 2sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% for each of the 4 vaccine strains (A/H1N1: -1.7%; A/H3N2: -8.1%; B/Yamagata: 9.5%; B/Victoria: 8.2%) (Table 17).

- o Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs US-Licensed QIVr

The  LL  of  the  2sided  97.5%  CI  for  the  adjusted  Day  29  GMT  ratio  was  ≥0.67  for  the  A/H1N1, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria strains, but not the A/H3N2 strain; the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% for each of the 4 vaccine strains. Therefore, the prespecified success criteria for demonstrating immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs US-licensed QIVr were not met in subjects aged 50 years and older.

Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs aQIV in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older

## o GMT Ratio

The LL of the 2sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was ≥0.67 for each of the 4 vaccine strains (A/H1N1: 1.43; A/H3N2: 1.15; B/Yamagata: 1.63; B/Victoria: 1.63)

## o SCR Difference

The LL of the 2sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% for each of the 4 vaccine strains (A/H1N1: 13.9%; A/H3N2: 6.4%; B/Yamagata: 21.3%; B/Victoria: 18.8%)

- o Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs aQIV

For each of the 4 vaccine strains, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was ≥0.67 and the LL of the 2 - sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% and, therefore, the prespecified success criteria for demonstrating immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs aQIV were met in subjects aged 50 years and older.

## Key-Secondary Immunogenicity Results

Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs aQIV in Subjects Aged 65 Years and Older

## o GMT Ratio

In subjects aged 65 years and older, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was  ≥0.67  for  each  of  the  4  vaccine  strains  (A/H1N1:  1.53;  A/H3N2:  1.18;  B/Yamagata:  1.66; B/Victoria: 1.67) (Table 18, Figure 7).

## o SCR Difference

In subjects aged 65 years and older, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10%  for  each  of  the  4  vaccine  strains  (A/H1N1:  16.8%;  A/H3N2:  7.5%;  B/Yamagata:  21.7%; B/Victoria: 19.6%) (Table 18)

- o Noninferiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs aQIV in Subjects Aged 65 Years and Older

For each of the 4 vaccine strains, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was ≥0.67 and the LL of the 2 - sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% and, therefore, the prespecified success criteria for demonstrating immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs aQIV were met in subjects aged 65 years and older.

Superiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs QIVr in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older

The superiority tests would only be conducted if noninferiority within the same comparator and within the same age group was concluded. As noninferiority was not concluded for all 4 vaccine strains for aQIVc HD vs QIVr in subjects aged 50 years and older, statistical testing of superiority of aQIVc HD vs QIVr was not conducted.

Superiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs aQIV in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older

## o GMT Ratio

The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was &gt;1.0 for each of the 4 vaccine strains (A/H1N1: 1.44; A/H3N2: 1.14; B/Yamagata: 1.62; B/Victoria: 1.62) (Table 19,

Figure 8).

## o Superiority Analysis of aQIVc HD vs aQIV

For each of the 4 vaccine strains, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was &gt;1.0 and, therefore, the prespecified success criteria for demonstrating immunological superiority of aQIVc HD vs aQIV were met in subjects aged 50 years and older.

## Other Secondary Immunogenicity Objectives

Superiority by Margin Analysis of aQIVc HD vs QIVr in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older

The superiority tests would only be conducted if noninferiority within the same comparator and within the same age group was concluded. As noninferiority was not concluded for aQIVc HD vs QIVr in subjects aged  50  years  and  older,  statistical  testing  of  superiority  by  margin  of  aQIVc  HD  vs  QIVr  was  not conducted.

Superiority by Margin Analysis of aQIVc HD vs aQIV in Subjects Aged 65 Years and Older

Superiority by margin would be demonstrated if the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the GMT ratio (aQIVc HD/aQIV) was &gt;1.5 for at least one of the two A strains.

The LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was &gt;1.5 for the A/H1N1 strain (1.53) and  below  1.5  for  the  A/H3N2  strain  (1.18).  Therefore,  the  prespecified  success  criteria  for demonstrating immunological superiority by margin of aQIVc HD vs aQIV were met in subjects aged 65 years and older.

Although not predefined in the success criteria for this objective, the superiority by margin criteria were also met for the two B strains (B/Yamagata: 1.65; B/Victoria: 1.65), Table 21.

Note 2: Day 29 GMTs and GMT ratios are adjusted values, calculated using an ANCOVA model with vaccine group, prevaccination titer (logio transformed), sex, country, and influenza vaccination history as covariates.

Note 3: Superiority by margin criteria for the GMT ratio: the lower limit of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 GMT ratio (shown in bold text) is &gt;1.5 for at least one of the two A strains.

Immunogenicity of aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV, Overall and by Age Subgroup

## o Overall population

For all 4 vaccine strains, the D29 GMTs, GMFIs , percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40 and SCRs in the aQIVc group were higher than those in the aQIV group.

Regarding the comparison of aQIVc vs QIVr, all endpoints (GMT, GMFIs , percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40 and SCRs), for H1N1 were similar, for H3N2 was either similar or lower in the aQIVc HD group and for both B strains, were higher in the aQIVc HD than in the QIVr group.

## o Age Subgroup Analyses

For all 4 vaccine strains, the D29 GMTs, GMFIs, percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40 and SCRs in the aQIVc group were higher than those in the aQIV group in both age subgroups.

Regarding the comparison of aQIVc vs QIVr, all endpoints (GMT, GMFIs , percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40 and SCRs), for H1N1 were similar, for H3N2 was either similar or lower in the aQIVc HD group and for both B strains, were higher in the aQIVc HD than in the QIVr group in both age subgroups.

Results were consistent between age groups.

Reverse Cumulative Distribution Curves (overall population)

The immune response profiles for the A/H1N1, A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria strains in the aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV groups are shown graphically for the PPS Immunogenicity using reverse cumulative distribution (RCD) curves. Only Day 29 curves are shown in this report (Figure 10).

For the A/H1N1 strain, the RCD curves mostly overlap, with the RCD curve for the QIVr group shifted slightly to the right relative to the RCD curve for the aQIVc HD group, which, in turn, is shifted slightly to the right relative to the RCD curve for the aQIV group.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

For the A/H3N2 strain, the RCD curve for the QIVr group is shifted to the right relative to those for the aQIVc HD and aQIV groups, suggesting a greater magnitude of immune response for this strain in the QIVr group.

For the B/Yamagata and B/Victoria strains, the RCD curve for the aQIVc HD group is shifted to the right relative to those for the QIVr and aQIV groups, suggesting a greater magnitude of immune response for the B strains in the aQIVc HD group.

Immunogenicity of aQIVc HD Compared with aQIV, Overall and by Age Subgroup, Using Egg-Derived Target Viruses

In the overall population, all endpoints were similar for the H3N2 and B/Victoria strains between the aQIVc  HD  and  the  aQIV  groups,  in  H1N1  similar  or  higher  in  the  aQIV  group,  and  higher  for  the B/Yamagata strain in the aQIVc HD group.

The immunogenicity results observed in the 5064 years and ≥65 years age subgroups based on the HI assay  using  egg-derived  target  viruses  were  consistent  with  those  observed  in  the  overall  study population.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Long-Term Immunogenicity of aQIVc HD vs QIVr and aQIV, Overall and by Age Subgroup

Analyses of Day 1 (retest), Day 29 (retest), Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365 HI GMTs, GMFIs, percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40, and SCRs, using cell -derived target viruses, have been presented for the aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV vaccine groups in the Long-Term subset.

In each of the three vaccine groups:

- Increases in HI GMTs were observed at Day 29 compared to Day 1 for all 4 vaccine strains
- The HI GMTs progressively decreased from Day 29 to Day 271 for each of the 4 vaccine strains
- The Day 365 HI GMTs remained elevated compared with the Day 1 HI GMTs for each of the 4 vaccine strains

For all estimands (GMTs, GMFIs, percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40, and SCRs), the comparison between aQIVc HD and QIVr was either similar or lower for H1N1 in the aQIVc HD group than in the QIVr group, lower for H3N2 in the aQIVc HD group and higher for the two B strains in the aQIVc HD group.

Regarding the comparison between aQIVc HD and aQIV, for all estimands (GMTs, GMFIs, percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40, and SCRs), for H1N1 and the two B strains, it was higher in the aQIVc HD group and lower for the H3N2 in the aQIVc HD than in the aQIV group.

## Age Subgroup Analyses in the Long-Term subset

- GMTs and percentage of subjects with HI titre ≥ 1:40 : In the aQIVc HD group, these point estimates at Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365 tended to be higher for the A/H1N1, A/H3N2, and B/Victoria strains in the ≥ 65 years age subgroup and higher for the B/Yamagata strain in the 50-64 years age subgroup.
- GMFI: In the aQIVc HD group, this point estimate at Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365 tended to be higher for the A/H3N2, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria strains in the 50-64 years age subgroup and higher for the A/H1N1 strain in the ≥ 65 years age subgroup.
- SCR: the Day 181, Day 271, and Day 365 this point estimate tended to be higher for the A/H1N1, B/Yamagata, and B/Victoria strains in the 50-64 years age subgroup and higher for the A/H3N2 strain in the ≥ 65 years age subgroup.

## Immunogenicity of aQIVc HD Compared with QIVr and aQIV in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older by MN Assay

Analyses of MN GMTs, GMFIs, and SCRs, using cell-derived target viruses, have been presented for the aQIVc HD, QIVr, and aQIV vaccine groups for a subset of the overall population (PPS-MN).

- GMTs and GMFIs (MN Assay): For the two A strains, the Day 29 GMTs and GMFIs in the aQIVc HD group were similar to (A/H1N1) or lower than (A/H3N2) those in the QIVr group. For the two B strains, the Day 29 GMTs and GMFIs in the aQIVc HD group were higher than those in the QIVr group.

For all 4 vaccine strains, the Day 29 GMTs and GMFIs in the aQIVc HD group were higher than those in the aQIV group.

- Seroconversion (MN Assay): The Day 29 SCRs in the aQIVc HD group were similar to or higher than those in the QIVr groups for the 4 vaccine strains.

For all 4 vaccine strains, the Day 29 SCRs in the aQIVc HD group were higher than those in the aQIV group.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Long-Term Immunogenicity of aQIVc HD Compared with QIVr and aQIV in Subjects Aged 50 Years and Older by MN Assay

Analyses of long-term immunogenicity by MN assay are currently ongoing; the results will be reported in a subsequent version of the CSR.

## Exploratory Immunogenicity Endpoints

Analyses of the exploratory immunogenicity endpoints are currently ongoing; the results will be reported in a subsequent version of the CSR.

## 5.3.2.1.4.5. Pre-defined and post-hoc subgroup analyses

## Subgroup Analyses for Immunogenicity

Immunogenicity analyses (Day 1 and Day 29 GMTs, GMT ratios, GMFIs, percentages of subjects with HI titre ≥1:40, SCRs, and SCR differences) stratified by the following variables were performed in the PPS Immunogenicity and FAS Immunogenicity: age, sex, influenza vaccination history, prevaccination titre, and country. These analyses were performed using cell-derived and egg-derived target viruses (for the HI assay) and also using cell-derived target viruses for the MN assay.

Only results from the PPS Immunogenicity population are included in this report, the complete set of data ara available in CSR V201\\_3 Version 2.0. Unless the egg-derived data present some additional interest, the data shown are only data coming from cell-derived target virus assays. The results and conclusions that are not shown in this report are in line with those included and discussed here.

## Age

The immune responses based on the HI assay using cell-derived target viruses are summarized for the 5064 years age subgroup and the ≥65 years age subgroup in Table 22. The higher immunogenicity of aQIVc HD at Day 29 compared to aQIV, was observed in both the 50-64 years age subgroup and the ≥65 years age subgroup for all strains.

For each of the 4 vaccine strains, the LL of the 2-sided 97.5% CI for the adjusted Day 29 GMT ratio was ≥0.67 and the LL of the 2 - sided 97.5% CI for the Day 29 SCR difference was ≥ -10% and, therefore, the prespecified success criteria for demonstrating immunological noninferiority of aQIVc HD vs aQIV were met in subjects aged 65 years and older.

Comparing both age groups, in general all point estimates at D29 tended to be higher in most strains in the 5064 age subgroup than in the ≥65 years age subgroup, with the exception of the GMTs in the eggderived virus HI, that were higher for three of the four strains in the ≥65 years age subgroup.

The results observed in the 50-64 years and ≥ 65 years age subgroups based on the HI assay using eggderived  target  viruses  are  summarized  in  Table  23.  Consistent  with  the  results  in  the  overall  study population, for 3 strains (A/H3N2 and the two B strains), immunogenicity was higher or comparable for aQIVc HD compared with aQIV in both age subgroups. The extent of this increased immunogenicity for aQIVc HD was slightly higher in the ≥ 65 years age subgroup compared to the 50-64 years age subgroup. For A/H1N1, values for the HI assay using egg-derived target viruses were lower for aQIVc HD compared with aQIV in both age groups, with higher values in the older age subgroup.
